1. **Identify the paths between \( R \) and \( J \):**
   - \( R \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow H \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)
   - \( R \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow H \rightarrow B \rightarrow S \rightarrow E \rightarrow A \rightarrow N \rightarrow C \rightarrow G \rightarrow H \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)

   - \( R \rightarrow J \)
   - \( R \rightarrow O \rightarrow Q \rightarrow I \rightarrow J \)

2. **Determine the minimal cut set to disconnect \( R \) and \( J \):**
   - Removing \( (R, J) \) directly disconnects one path.
   - Removing \( (R, G) \) eliminates the path through \( G \).
   - Removing \( (O, Q) \) or \( (Q, I) \) eliminates the path through \( O \) and \( Q \).

To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (E and J), we need to analyze the paths between them. Let's identify the paths and find the critical nodes whose removal will break all possible connections.

### Step-by-Step Analysis:

1. **Identify Paths from E to J:**
   - Direct paths from E to J:
     - E → B → T → N → H → I → A → P → I → Q → J
     - E → B → T → N → H → I → Q → J
     - E → B → S → Q → J

2. **Remove Critical Nodes:**
   - **Node B:** Removing B disconnects the paths E → B → T → N → H → I → A → P → I → Q → J and E → B → T → N → H → I → Q → J.
   - **Node S:** Removing S disconnects the path E → B → S → Q → J.
   - **Node Q:** Removing Q disconnects the paths E → B → S → Q → J, E → B → T → N → H → I → Q → J, and E → B → T → N → H → I → A → P → I → Q → J.

### Conclusion:
Removing nodes B and Q will ensure that all paths between E and J are disconnected. However, we need to check if there is a more minimal solution.

### Further Analysis:
- Removing **Node B** and **Node S** disconnects all paths except those going through Q.
- Removing **Node B** and **Node Q** disconnects all paths except those going through S.
- Removing **Node S** and **Node Q** disconnects all paths except those going through B.

Thus, removing both **Node B** and either **Node S** or **Node Q** is sufficient.

### Final Answer:
Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, S]

{'length': 1, 'cut_set': [['H'], ['I']]}

- From E, we explore all direct connections: A, B, F, S, T, J, H.
- From each of these nodes, we explore their connections:
  - A connects to: F, E, B, S (already explored or not leading towards R)
  - B connects to: E, A, F, S, T, J, H
  - F connects to: A, B, S, C (already explored or not leading towards R)
  - S connects to: F, C, G, A, E, B, T (already explored or not leading towards R)
  - T connects to: B, S, G, I (not leading towards R directly), Q
  - J connects to: B, H, E (already explored or not leading towards R)
  - H connects to: J, B, E (already explored)

Next, we explore the connections from node T (since T is connected to Q and I):

- Q connects to: O, I, R (connected)
- I connects to: T, Q, R (already explored)

We find the shortest path is from T to Q to R.

Thus, the shortest path from E to R is:
E -> T -> Q -> R

Length: 3, Path: [E, T, Q, R]
